# Fusion Spec Completion Matrix - 2026-06-07

This matrix tracks completion against the four 2026-06-04 fusion design specs:

- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-complete-fusion-design.md`
- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-complete-fusion-design-part2.md`
- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-complete-fusion-design-part3.md`
- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-functional-logic-details.md`

Status values:

- `Proven`: current files plus focused tests/verification cover the requirement.
- `Partial`: current files show implementation exists, but requirement coverage is incomplete or not fully verified.
- `Gap`: current evidence shows missing behavior.
- `Unverified`: code may exist, but evidence is too weak for a completion claim.

Streaming Agent messages now honor default planning mode by creating a durable planning run, streaming the planning reply, persisting parsed plan steps, and avoiding pre-approval tool execution; they also honor explicit ReAct/planning runtime mode overrides while rejecting invalid stream mode overrides before persistence. Failed plan-step retry now preserves the approval boundary: failed steps that are already approved or not-required still retry immediately only after prior steps are completed or skipped, out-of-order retry rejection leaves old run/step failure evidence intact, while failed steps that still require approval reopen as `pending` with stale result/error cleared and do not execute until approved. Manual planning draft creation and editing now reuse the same Agent approval policy as model-generated plan steps: safe or no-tool drafts can remain `not_required`, medium or dangerous tool edits reopen as `pending`, and stale pending `not_required` tool steps are rechecked at execution time before any tool or model step can run. | Partial | Remaining proof is external: target deployed Agent compatibility, target gRPC smoke, target secret audit, workflow telemetry, and final no-skip release proof. |
| Multi-channel publishing | Functional logic 5.1-5.3; Part 2 3.7 | Publishing channel package, platform adapters, worker, message transformer, logs, routes, and workspace publishing UI exist. Automatic due-retry processing now shares the same channel health threshold as manual sends: consecutive retry successes recover degraded channels to active, consecutive retry failures degrade active channels, and retry-triggered degradation routes through the publishing-channel alert/recovery path. Message logs now have a default 30-day configurable retention policy, a batched expired-log archive/delete store path that preserves retry queues, and a configured archive worker that writes expired message-log objects before deleting DB rows. Archive storage now supports both the legacy file/object-root sink and a native S3-compatible sink for MinIO/S3 using `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_LOG_ARCHIVE_BACKEND=s3|minio` plus endpoint, bucket, region, and credentials; focused channel/config/http tests prove SigV4 PUT payloads and server sink selection. Workspace operator controls now expose one-click fallback switching for failed queues: selecting a fallback channel sends `force=true`, immediately claims current-channel retry-pending messages even before their retry window, and routes them through the fallback channel. Administrator degradation notifications now carry channel name, failure type, impact scope, failure reason, and message-log context in both in-app notification metadata/message and alert events; focused HTTP tests prove delivery through email, in-app, and third-party webhook channels plus retry-worker degradation payloads. Both the active publishing adapter registry and the legacy `ChannelService` adapter package now have focused coverage for Feishu interactive card outbound payloads plus WeChat news-card and image `media_id` outbound payloads. The legacy `ChannelService` adapter map now registers Telegram and WebEmbed adapters by default, and focused tests prove their outbound text/SDK payloads through `SendMessage`. Active and legacy Discord adapters now preserve inbound reaction metadata and emit outbound emoji/reaction payloads from internal metadata. Active and legacy WeChat adapters now support XML inbound/outbound when requested while keeping existing JSON payload compatibility. | Proven | No known repository-owned gaps for Functional Logic 5.1-5.3; continue the top-level matrix with Billing and monetization. |
| Billing and monetization | Functional logic 6.1-6.3; Part 2 3.5 | Billing, quota, payment, Stripe, pricing settings, API token, usage log and analytics files exist across server/admin/web. Usage-limit settings now include `maxTokensPerRequest`; quota resolution maps it into Relay rate-limit checks; in-memory and Redis rate limiters reject oversized single requests with the `request_tokens` dimension before consuming RPM/TPM windows; Relay routing now reserves configured concurrent-request leases before consuming RPM/TPM windows, releases already-begun leases if a later concurrent key is full or an RPM/TPM/request-token check fails, rejects concurrent `Begin` failures before upstream/billing/API-token preauthorization, and releases leases on quota preconsume failure, API-token preauthorization failure, and retried upstream errors. Admin settings APIs and UI types preserve the field; Admin Settings now shows usage-limit runtime enforcement/recovery signals by correlating each configured limit with recent usage-log 429/`relay_rate_limited` hits and latest successful recovery traffic; migration `0071_token_rate_limit_request_cap.sql` upgrades existing `token_rate_limits` rows. Subscription packages now also carry `maxTokensPerRequest`; Admin plan create/update/list surfaces preserve it; migration `0072_packages_request_token_cap.sql` upgrades existing packages; quota resolution falls back to the active subscription package request cap when no explicit org/user usage-limit cap is configured. Domestic payment checkout providers now have deployment configuration: `ALIPAY_CHECKOUT_BASE_URL` and `WECHATPAY_CHECKOUT_BASE_URL` enable hosted Alipay/WeChat Pay checkout creators, register the providers as configured CNY payment methods, and route subscription/top-up checkout through the same tenant payment-intent ledger used by Stripe. Domestic payment webhooks now have `ALIPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`/`WECHATPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, public Alipay/WeChat Pay webhook routes, HMAC-SHA256 verification over `timestamp + "." + raw_body`, invalid-signature rejection, idempotent provider event ledger recording, and `checkout.paid` lifecycle mapping into the existing checkout completion state machine so duplicate domestic events do not double-apply top-up credit. Domestic subscription `subscription.updated` and `subscription.deleted` events now map into the shared subscription lifecycle without requiring a local payment intent ID; duplicate Alipay subscription events update the local subscription status/customer/cancel-at-period-end state once and record provider-specific `alipay.subscription.*` lifecycle transitions. Domestic marketplace install `checkout.paid` events now carry `marketplace_order_id` through the domestic webhook adapter and apply through the marketplace settlement applier instead of the generic checkout store, so duplicate Alipay paid-install events complete one order, one install, one settlement, and one lifecycle transition. Domestic refund events now map `refund.succeeded`, `refund.created`, and `payment.refunded` into the shared refund lifecycle with provider-specific refund IDs; focused tests prove duplicate Alipay refund events record one provider refund, reverse top-up quota once, and apply marketplace-install refund impact to `marketplace_orders` and `marketplace_settlements` through the settlement applier. Domestic payout events now map `payout.paid` and `payout.failed` through the domestic webhook adapter into marketplace payout lifecycle handling; route-level PostgreSQL tests cover duplicate Alipay paid/failed payout events updating marketplace payout and settlement state through the SQL webhook ledger when a test database is configured. Admin Billing now also has a manual provider-payout failure operator path: pending payouts can be marked failed with provider payout ID plus reason, the shared payout lifecycle releases pending settlements back to available, the route is CSRF/OpenAPI guarded, and the Admin Billing UI exposes the action alongside manual paid confirmation. Usage analytics now covers model/feature/user/time, model-time/user-feature/feature-time cross buckets, and channel/provider cost buckets across the SQL and ClickHouse stores, service response, frontend API types, and Admin Usage Logs panels; analytics granularity now accepts second/minute/hour/day/week/month, with week/month rolled up from PostgreSQL daily aggregates and mapped to native ClickHouse week/month bucket functions. PostgreSQL daily cost aggregates now have migration `0074_usage_daily_aggregates.sql`, a `RefreshUsageDailyAggregates` upsert path from `usage_records`, day/week/month-granularity SQL analytics reads from `usage_daily_aggregates`, and the aggregate source uses `total_tokens`/`total_cost` expressions while raw usage analytics keeps the raw record expressions. User-level quota balance isolation now has migration `0073_user_quota_balance_scope.sql`, SQL/fake store scope resolution, organization/user partial unique indexes, user-mode PreConsume/Topup balance separation, admin user-scope quota updates, and Stripe/domestic top-up lifecycle updates pinned to organization-scope quota rows. App-facing quota balance, active package list, billing checkout CSRF/schema contract, Console API token create/revoke CSRF/schema contract, and legacy top-up compatibility routes are now documented in OpenAPI and enforced by the contract gate; the top-up compatibility response points callers to `/api/v1/billing/checkout` with `kind=topup`. ## 2026-06-16 Matrix Row Updates

- API gateway and Relay row / Security row 33 / Release readiness row 34: Relay file list passthrough now has tenant-scoped ownership proof instead of production-disabled raw upstream passthrough. `GET /v1/files` requires the configured file mapping store, trusted user and organization identity, and a list-capable mapping store; it loads only current-tenant mappings from `relay_file_mappings`, skips upstream when the tenant has no mappings, filters upstream file-list data to mapped provider file IDs, rewrites `id` to the local file ID, preserves `provider_file_id`, and clears pagination metadata that could imply hidden upstream files. ## 2026-06-13 Matrix Row Updates

- Frontend row 28 / Marketplace row 27: Workspace shell navigation now keeps Marketplace inside the `Workspace navigation` landmark and marks it active for parameterized Marketplace detail routes such as `/marketplace/agents/:agentId`. Focused layout and real app-router tests prove the Marketplace link is discoverable from the semantic workspace nav, carries `aria-current="page"` on agent detail pages, and still reaches the Marketplace browse/install flow inside the Workspace shell.
- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28: Workspace Agent plan-step UI now respects the persisted run execution mode when rendering planning-only controls. React-mode pending-approval runs keep tool approval/reject actions available, but `Continue plan`, plan adjustment, and plan-step create/edit/move/approve/execute/skip/retry/delete controls are disabled once run detail reports `mode=react`; focused page coverage proves the dual-engine UI boundary without weakening the existing backend service and HTTP guards.
- Agent row 24: Long-term Agent memory `memory_key_consolidate` now treats response-language preference as a stable preference slot instead of preserving conflicting "answer in English" and "reply in Chinese" memories as unrelated content-derived preference records. Existing legacy response-language preference keys are recomputed into `preference:response_language` only for that stable slot, while unrelated preference keys still remain content-derived; focused runner tests prove the old memory updates in place with consolidation metadata.
- Frontend row 28 / Workflow row 22: `/workflows` now has a real Playwright browser journey in addition to app-router and page-level tests. `src/web/e2e/workflows.spec.ts` uses `src/web/e2e/fixtures/workflows.ts` to exercise the actual Workspace shell, active Workflows navigation, React Flow canvas, signed webhook helper, conversation and semantic trigger matching, manual workflow execution, webhook-triggered execution, scheduled-task run feedback, node debug execution, execution list loading, and execution debug snapshot rendering. This is local frontend regression evidence only; it does not reclassify Workflow row 22's remaining external production success-rate proof.
- Security row 33 / Release readiness row 34: dependency-security now has a clean local gate. The frontend toolchain moved to `vite@8.0.16` and `@vitejs/plugin-react@6.0.2`, removing the audited `esbuild@0.25.x` path from both `pnpm-lock.yaml` and the tracked `src/web/package-lock.json`, and the unused root `wireit` devDependency was removed from `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, and `pnpm-lock.yaml`, clearing the remaining `brace-expansion@4.0.1` advisory path. GitHub Dependabot Go manifest alerts were also remediated by upgrading `google.golang.org/grpc` to `v1.79.3` and `filippo.io/edwards25519` to `v1.1.1`. `bash scripts/check.sh security` passed with `pnpm audit` reporting no known npm vulnerabilities and `govulncheck v1.3.0` reporting the code is affected by 0 vulnerabilities; `npm --prefix src/web audit --audit-level=moderate --registry=https://registry.npmjs.org` also passed with 0 vulnerabilities for the web npm lockfile. This reduces release risk but does not reclassify Security or Release readiness because target-environment, DB-backed, and broader isolation evidence still remain.

## 2026-06-11 Matrix Row Updates

- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Agent planning-only run actions now fail closed for ReAct-mode runs at the HTTP boundary. `AdjustPlanSteps` and `ContinuePlanningRun` already reject non-planning runs before model calls, plan-step execution, assistant-message writes, plan-step replacement, or run mutation; `writeAgentWorkflowError` now maps that rejection to `409 invalid_state` instead of an internal error. Focused service and handler tests cover `adjust-plan` and `continue-plan` against a pending-approval ReAct run and prove the guarded run/message/step evidence remains unchanged.
- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Concrete Agent plan-step actions now fail closed for ReAct-mode runs at the shared service boundary. Approve, update, move, delete, execute, skip, retry, and create plan-step paths reject non-planning runs before model/tool execution, tool-run creation, run updates, or plan-step mutation; focused service coverage proves stale/synthetic ReAct plan steps remain unchanged while existing planning-mode edit, execution, retry, and skip behavior remains intact.
- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Agent tool-run approval and retry actions now also validate the parent run state at the service boundary, not only through HTTP selection filters. Approve/reject require the parent run to still be `pending_approval`, retry only accepts failed or still pending-approval recovery states, and stale matching tool-run actions against running, completed, max-iteration, or token-budget-stopped runs return `409 invalid_state` without mutating run, message, or tool-run evidence.
- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Agent plan-step actions now validate the parent planning run state at the shared service boundary. Approve/create/update/move/delete/execute/skip require the parent run to still be `pending_approval`, retry only accepts failed, token-budget-exceeded, or still-open planning runs, and stale plan-step actions against running, completed, failed, max-iteration, or token-budget-stopped runs return `409 invalid_state` without mutating run, plan-step, tool-run, or message evidence.
- Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31: Console Notifications now consumes the existing unread-count and mark-all-read backend surfaces. The frontend API exposes `GET /api/v1/app/notifications/unread-count` and `POST /api/v1/app/notifications/mark-all-read`; the Console Notifications page renders the server unread count, refreshes it after single-notification reads, and adds a mark-all-read operator action with focused API and page coverage.
- Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Console Notifications now consumes the existing single-notification delete surface. The frontend API exposes `DELETE /api/v1/app/notifications/{notificationId}`; the Console Notifications page adds per-notification delete controls, refreshes the list and server unread count after deletion, and focused API/page/router tests cover the route usage without weakening the existing CSRF-protected backend contract.
- Marketplace row 27 / Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Marketplace agent detail now consumes the existing user-governance routes. The frontend API exposes `POST /api/v1/marketplace/agents/{agentId}/appeal` and `POST /api/v1/marketplace/agents/{agentId}/abuse-reports`; the detail page adds appeal and abuse-report actions with required-reason gating, success/error states, and focused API/page tests, while backend session, CSRF, and publisher/report authorization remain enforced by the existing HTTP route surface.
- Marketplace row 27 / Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Real app-router regression coverage now exercises the recent Marketplace/Admin governance controls through `/marketplace/my-agents` and `/admin/reviews`. The route-level tests create a template through the My Agents Template Factory, confirm publisher delete through the destructive dialog, run review SLA enforcement, resolve an abuse report with operator evidence, and apply takedown/reinstate governance actions inside the real Workspace/Admin shells.
- Multi-channel publishing row 25 / Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workspace Publishing Channels now consumes the existing full-channel update and delete routes. The frontend API exposes `PUT /api/v1/channels/{channelId}` and `DELETE /api/v1/channels/{channelId}`; the workspace page adds edit and delete actions that preserve redacted secret markers for backend restoration, update list state from the returned channel, and focused API/page tests cover the route usage without weakening the CSRF-protected backend contract.
- Multi-channel publishing row 25 / Frontend row 28: Real app-router regression coverage now exercises the Publishing channel edit/delete workflow inside the Workspace shell. The route-level test opens `/publishing`, edits an existing channel through the full-channel update form, observes returned list-state replacement, then deletes the renamed channel and verifies the disabled-channel response remains visible in the configured-channel list.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: API route-surface parity is now manifest-driven. `docs/api/route-surface-manifest.json` enumerates every current `/api/` OpenAPI operation with method, path template, concrete sample path, security mode, CSRF expectation, operation ID, and tags; `scripts/verify-openapi-contract.sh` now resolves OpenAPI Path Item `$ref` entries such as Knowledge aliases and requires the manifest to match OpenAPI bidirectionally. `TestRouteSurfaceManifestRoutesAreRegisteredWithoutDatabase` loads the same manifest and proves all 342 operations reach the production runtime handler surface without falling through to route-level 404/405, including Relay aliases through `combineHandlers`.
- Billing row 26 / Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Real app-router regression coverage now exercises Admin Billing operator money-movement workflows inside the protected Admin shell. The route-level tests open `/admin/billing`, switch to the Payouts and Top-ups tabs, create due Marketplace payouts, mark a pending payout paid, mark a pending payout failed with operator reason evidence, and record a top-up refund with provider refund/charge/payment-intent evidence through the Admin API mocks.

- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Agent tool-decision route guards now have explicit DB-free coverage for additional approval-bypass variants: reject/retry actions reject omitted `toolRunId` when multiple matching tool runs exist, and explicit reject/retry requests cannot target a tool run from a different URL run. The tests assert guarded tool-run state is not mutated, complementing the existing approve, non-pending, and retry-selection coverage.
- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: Agent plan-step mutation route guards now have explicit DB-free coverage for cross-run and ambiguous-selection variants across skip/retry/update/move/delete/execute, plus approve for omitted ambiguous selections. Explicit `planStepId` values from a different URL run return `400 invalid_request`, omitted IDs with multiple matching plan steps return the existing ambiguity error, and the tests assert guarded steps are neither mutated nor deleted.
- Frontend row 28 / Knowledge row 22: Real app-router regression coverage now exercises `/knowledge/kb_router` beyond reachability, proving the Workspace shell, route parameter API calls, knowledge-base RAG config readback, document/version controls, RAG citation metadata, selected-chunk preview/edit/split/merge controls, and retrieval test execution remain reachable in the actual router path.

- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28 / Security row 33: Agent planning `adjust-plan` now has the same run-state approval boundary as continuation. - Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Admin user quota updates now have explicit operator-boundary coverage. `PATCH /api/v1/admin/users/{userId}` requires an admin session, rejects missing or negative/non-finite balances before the store, verifies the target user exists before writing user-scope quota, records `user.quota.update` audit evidence with actor/IP/balance, returns stable `user not found` errors instead of leaking SQL text, and the OpenAPI contract now locks `AdminUserQuotaUpdateRequest.balance` as required and non-negative.
- Billing row 26 / Marketplace row 27 / API contract row 31: Admin Billing Marketplace settlement inspection now applies the already-documented `provider` and `kind` filters through the money-movement chain: settlement summary/count/list queries join `marketplace_settlements` to `marketplace_orders` and `payment_intents`, so `provider=stripe|alipay|wechatpay` filters by the checkout provider that created the Marketplace install. The handler returns `501 provider_not_configured` before creating settlement/payment-order state, before recording checkout sessions or failures, and without falling back to another provider's checkout creator.

Focused handler tests now assert token list and usage responses do not expose `rawToken`, `tokenHash`, or `token_hash` while preserving organization/user/token scoping.

- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31: Admin Billing operator mutation APIs now fail closed when required provider evidence is missing: top-up refunds require provider, provider refund ID, positive amount, currency, and Stripe charge/payment-intent evidence; payout-paid marking requires a non-empty provider payout ID. - Billing row 26: Admin top-up refund operator validation now requires domestic Alipay/WeChat Pay refunds to include provider payment-intent evidence in addition to provider refund ID, amount, and currency; focused service and handler tests prove missing domestic evidence is rejected before the admin store while valid domestic evidence is forwarded.
- Billing row 26: Top-up refund lifecycle state is now consistent across Admin operator refunds and provider webhook refunds: both paths update `topup_orders.status` to `partially_refunded`/`refunded` alongside `refunded_amount`, so Admin top-up lists and status filters no longer show refunded top-ups as still only `paid`. DB-free SQL contract tests guard both update paths, and DB-backed Admin/Stripe/Alipay integration assertions are in place for environments with `TEST_DATABASE_URL`.
- Marketplace row 27 / API contract row 31: paid Marketplace installs now fail closed when the caller omits a payment provider instead of silently falling back to the registry default; the OpenAPI contract gate documents both free-install and paid-install request bodies and locks `MarketplacePaidInstallRequest.provider` as required.
- Marketplace row 27 / Frontend row 28: Marketplace paid-agent detail pages now explain when no checkout providers are configured, hide the provider selector, and keep the install action disabled instead of leaving a silent disabled button.
- Marketplace row 27: paid Marketplace install provider handling now has DB-free unsupported-provider coverage: requesting an unregistered provider returns the shared `unsupported_provider` error before settlement checkout or checkout-creator dispatch, complementing the existing missing, unconfigured, and configured provider proofs.
- Marketplace row 27 / Billing row 26: paid Marketplace install checkout creator failure now has DB-free fail-closed coverage: after a provider checkout error, the handler returns `checkout_create_failed`, marks the marketplace order/payment intent failed through the settlement service, preserves provider/order/agent/version/publisher metadata in the attempted checkout request, and does not record a provider checkout session.
- Marketplace row 27 / Billing row 26: domestic hosted checkout configuration now feeds Marketplace paid-agent discovery, so configuring Alipay and WeChat Pay checkout base URLs exposes `stripe`, `alipay`, and `wechatpay` in agent detail `paymentProviders` only when each provider also has a checkout creator.
- Billing row 26: Billing checkout provider rejection now has DB-free fail-before-artifact coverage: unconfigured Alipay/WeChat Pay checkout returns `provider_not_configured`, unsupported providers such as PayPal return `unsupported_provider`, and both paths avoid payment-intent creation, top-up order creation, checkout-session recording, and checkout-creator dispatch.
- Billing row 26: Admin Billing payment-intent inspection now has DB-free handler evidence that `organizationId`/`organizationID`, `userID`/`userId`, `status`, `kind`, `provider`, `limit`, and `offset` query parameters are parsed, clamped, and passed into the shared `BillingInspectionFilter` before the Admin store call.
- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Console API token create/revoke OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader`, require `CreateRelayAPITokenRequest`, and return `CreatedRelayAPIToken` from create responses; DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie token create/revoke mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Tenant organization mutation OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for organization select, member role update/removal, invitation create/revoke/accept, and ownership transfer routes; the contract gate also locks key Tenant request body schemas, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie organization mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Tenant organization read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for organization membership list and member-list reads; the contract gate locks `OrganizationMembership` arrays in `memberships` and `members` response payloads, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous requests return 401 before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Tenant organization response schema fidelity now uses named response wrappers for membership-list, member-list, and ownership-transfer responses; the contract gate locks `OrganizationMembershipListResponse`, `OrganizationMembersResponse`, and `OrganizationOwnershipTransferResponse` instead of allowing inline object drift.
- Workflow row 23 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workflow execution control OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for resource-check, failure decision, pause, resume, and cancel routes; the contract gate locks their execution response and request body schemas, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie execution control mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Task mutation OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for create, start, approve, pause, resume, cancel, and budget update routes; the contract gate locks `Task` responses plus create/budget request bodies, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie task mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31: Task schema fidelity now documents the runtime `standard`/`safe`/`auto` execution modes, authorization scopes, `TaskDetail` response payloads for detail/action routes, and structured task steps/events/result artifacts. The contract gate rejects legacy `semi-auto`/`manual` task execution-mode enums and locks create versus detail/action response refs; focused Task service coverage proves legacy modes normalize to `standard` while `auto`, `safe`, and `standard` remain the persisted runtime modes.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Notification mutation OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for create, mark-all-read, mark-read, and delete routes; the contract gate locks notification/action response schemas and create request body schema, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie notification mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Scheduled Tasks OpenAPI contracts now document list, create, runs, status, and run-now routes; the contract gate locks `ScheduledTask`, `ScheduledTaskRun`, create/status request schemas, typed list/run responses, and `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for create/status/run mutations; DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie Scheduled Task mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution and dispatch through the real router when CSRF is present.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Task and Scheduled Task read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for task list/detail plus scheduled-task list/runs reads; the contract gate locks typed read response schemas, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous requests return 401 before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Preferences and Notification read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for preferences get plus notification list and unread-count reads; the contract gate locks Preferences, Notification[], and NotificationUnreadCount response schemas, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous requests return 401 before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Preferences update OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for `PUT /api/v1/app/me/preferences`; the contract gate locks `UpdatePreferencesRequest` and `Preferences` response schemas, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie preference updates without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat mutation OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for conversation create, send-message, config update, and convert-to-task routes; the contract gate locks create/send/config request schemas, `Conversation`, `Message[]`, `ConversationConfig`, and `TaskDraft` response schemas, and the HTTP handler now persists `personaId` from config updates into the Chat service path.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat private read route-surface coverage now proves models, conversation list/detail, message list, conversation config, and markdown export routes return 401 without a session before handler/database execution, while public share reads remain unauthenticated.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat private read OpenAPI contracts now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for models, persona list/detail, conversation list/detail, message list, config, and markdown export reads; the contract gate preserves response schema/media-type checks and keeps public share reads declared as `security: []`.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: WebSocket `/api/v1/ws` is now documented as a cookie-authenticated Realtime upgrade route; the OpenAPI contract gates GET, `cookieAuth` without CSRF, `101` upgrade, `401`, and `405` responses, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous GET returns 401 and unsupported methods return 405 before database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33 / Frontend row 28: Chat share OpenAPI contracts now document conversation-share and message-share creation, public message/conversation share reads, share expiration/range request schemas, share response/detail payloads, `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` on share creation, and `security: []` on public share reads; DB-free route-surface coverage proves signed-cookie share creation is rejected without CSRF and public share reads dispatch without a session.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat conversation detail CRUD now has OpenAPI and contract-gate parity for `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/app/conversations/{conversationId}`; the contract gate locks `Conversation`, `UpdateConversationRequest`, and `ConversationDeleteResponse`, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves signed-cookie conversation update/delete mutations reject missing CSRF before handler/database execution.
- Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat conversation fork now has front-to-back field parity and OpenAPI coverage. The workspace API client translates existing UI `messageId` calls into `branchFromMessageId`, the handler remains compatible with legacy `messageId`, the contract gate locks `ForkConversationRequest`, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves signed-cookie fork mutations reject missing CSRF before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat message action routes now have OpenAPI and contract-gate parity for update, delete, and bookmark alongside message share; DB-free route-surface coverage proves signed-cookie message mutations reject missing CSRF before handler/database execution, while public share reads remain unauthenticated.
- Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat stream/export helper routes now have behavior and contract parity. `POST /api/v1/app/conversations/{conversationId}/messages/stream` emits real `text/event-stream` SSE frames through `SendMessageStream` while preserving assistant persistence, usage recording, and semantic workflow triggers; `GET /api/v1/app/conversations/{conversationId}/export.md` returns `text/markdown`; the OpenAPI contract gates both response media types plus stream CSRF/request schema, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves missing-CSRF stream requests are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Chat conversation config read now has typed OpenAPI response parity: `GET /api/v1/app/conversations/{conversationId}/config` returns `ConversationConfig` inside the standard envelope, and the contract gate rejects regression back to a bare `Envelope` response.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Auth password-reset public routes now have OpenAPI and contract-gate parity: `/api/v1/auth/password-reset/request` and `/api/v1/auth/password-reset/confirm` declare `security: []`, typed request schemas, and typed Envelope `data` responses; DB-free route-surface coverage proves the public routes dispatch to JSON validation without requiring a session or database.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Auth logout now has typed response parity: `POST /api/v1/auth/logout` requires `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader`, returns `AuthLogoutResponse` inside the standard envelope, and the session CSRF contract gate locks the `loggedOut` boolean.
- API contract row 31: OpenAPI success response schema fidelity now has a repository-wide gate: `/api/` non-Relay `2xx application/json` responses must keep object-shaped `data` payloads behind named component schemas instead of inline anonymous objects.
- API contract row 31: OpenAPI request-body schema fidelity now has a repository-wide gate: `/api/` JSON request bodies must use named component schemas, with an explicit allowlist only for external webhook payloads that intentionally accept arbitrary provider/channel/workflow event objects.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33 / Frontend row 28: Chat persona CRUD routes are now mounted through the canonical Chat registrar, documented for list/create/detail/update/delete, and guarded by the OpenAPI contract for typed `Persona`, `PersonaRequest`, and delete response schemas plus `cookieAuth` and `csrfHeader` on persona mutations; DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie persona mutations without CSRF are rejected and valid-CSRF persona CRUD dispatches without a database.
- Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31: Chat persona CRUD is now usable from workspace Conversation settings; the Chat API client creates, updates, deletes, and normalizes persona payloads, while the Chat page can create personas from multiline question drafts, edit existing personas, and delete a selected persona while clearing the conversation config. Focused API and page behavior tests cover these flows.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Admin organization OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for organization create, update, and archive routes; the contract gate locks organization list/detail/member wrapper schemas plus create/update request bodies, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-admin-cookie organization mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Knowledge/RAG OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader` for knowledge base, document, chunk, retrieval, upload, and retrieval-test-case mutations; the contract gate locks KnowledgeBase/document config metadata, multipart upload, typed retrieval results, retrieval-test-case schemas, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie Knowledge mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.

- API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Knowledge/RAG compatibility aliases under `/api/v1/knowledge-bases...` and `/api/v1/documents/{documentId}` are now mounted through the main router, documented in OpenAPI, contract-gated to their canonical schemas or root document delete contract, and covered by DB-free route-surface tests for anonymous 401, signed-cookie missing-CSRF 403, and valid-CSRF dispatch.
- API contract row 31 / Frontend row 28: Console model summaries now have typed OpenAPI response fidelity: `/api/v1/console/models` returns `ModelSummary[]` inside the standard envelope, the contract gate locks `id`, `label`, and non-negative `requests`, and a handler test proves the route returns organization-scoped model summaries.

- Agent row 24 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workspace Agent read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for agent list/detail, enabled tools, conversation list/detail, messages, runs, and run detail reads; the contract gate locks response schemas, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous workspace Agent reads return 401 before handler/database execution.

- Memory row 25 / Agent row 24 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workspace Memory document/search and Agent memory create/import/update/delete OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader`; the contract gate locks memory document/search and agent memory request/response refs, reusable delete/update schemas, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie memory mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Legacy quota top-up OpenAPI now requires `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader`, locks `QuotaTopupRequest`, and documents the payment-required `Envelope` response; DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie direct top-up without CSRF is rejected and that valid-CSRF requests dispatch to the compatibility 402 path.
- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: App quota and package read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for quota balance and active package-list reads; the contract gate locks `QuotaSnapshot` and `PackageOption[]` response schemas, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous requests return 401 before handler/database execution.
- Workflow row 23 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workflow management, trigger matching, session webhook, execution start, branch publish/merge, rollback, and node-test OpenAPI contracts now require `cookieAuth` plus `csrfHeader`; the contract gate locks request/response refs while preserving the public signed webhook `security: []`, and DB-free route-surface tests prove signed-cookie Workflow mutations without CSRF are rejected before handler/database execution.
- Workflow row 23 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Workflow read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for workflow list/detail, version history, execution list/detail, and execution debug snapshot reads; the contract gate locks their typed envelope response refs while preserving the public signed webhook boundary, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous Workflow reads return 401 before handler/database execution.
- Marketplace row 27 / API contract row 31: domestic payout-provider webhooks now have an OpenAPI contract gate for Alipay and WeChat Pay public webhook security, signature headers, `DomesticPaymentWebhookEvent` payout enums/fields, and `WebhookReceivedResponse` data refs, protecting the payout lifecycle payload surface from schema drift.
- Billing row 26: Billing top-up checkout now fails closed when the configured provider checkout creator fails after local intent/order creation: `payment_intents` and `topup_orders` are marked `failed`, provider checkout session IDs remain empty, no quota credit is issued, and the HTTP response remains `502 checkout_create_failed`.
- Marketplace row 27: Paid Marketplace install checkout now fails closed when the configured provider checkout creator fails after local order/intent creation: `marketplace_orders` and linked `payment_intents` are marked `failed`, no install or settlement is created, and the HTTP response remains `502 checkout_create_failed`; migration `0078_marketplace_order_failed_status.sql` allows the explicit failed order state.
- Billing row 26 / Marketplace row 27 / API contract row 31: Billing checkout and Marketplace paid-install OpenAPI contracts now document provider-not-configured `501` and checkout-create-failed `502` standard `Envelope` responses, with `scripts/verify-openapi-contract.sh` gating those status/schema contracts.
- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31: Console read surfaces now have typed OpenAPI fidelity for usage, access, invoices, API token lists, and API token usage history. `UsageSummary` documents period/request counts, usage dimensions, time series, and recent token usage; `AccessSummary` documents session/user/preference fields; and the contract gate locks the corresponding response refs plus token-list secrecy.
- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Console read routes now explicitly require `cookieAuth` without CSRF for usage, access, models, billing, invoices, API token list, and API token usage reads; the contract gate locks the read-route auth/tag contract while preserving typed response schemas, and DB-free route-surface coverage proves anonymous Console reads return 401 before handler/database execution.
- Marketplace row 27 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Admin Marketplace review contracts now gate typed list/SLA/status response data refs, reject/needs-changes decision request bodies, and the review schema shapes while preserving the existing cookie-plus-CSRF mutation requirement.

- Billing row 26 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Console API token revoke now has a named `RelayAPITokenRevokeResponse` schema, a gated DELETE response data ref, and DB-free handler proof that revoke returns `status=revoked` while preserving organization/user/token scoping.
- Agent row 24: Workspace Agent plan-step UI now consumes full run-detail responses from tool approve/reject/retry decisions, so approval resume updates run status, stop reason, iteration/tool-call counts, plan steps, and tool runs in one state sync instead of only replacing the tool queue.
- Agent row 24 / Security row 33: failed tool-run retry now preserves the approval boundary for tools that are still `approvalStatus=pending`; retry reopens the run and tool run as pending approval, clears stale failure evidence, and does not execute or increment attempts until the user approves.
- Agent row 24: Approved tool execution now reopens the durable run with `ClearCompletedAt=true` before resuming the ReAct loop, so SQL-backed runs cannot carry stale terminal `completed_at` evidence into the next pending-approval pause.
- Agent row 24: Successful `retry-tool` recovery now has request-level proof that the failed run is reopened with `ClearCompletedAt=true` and a cleared error before the ReAct loop resumes, preventing stale terminal evidence from surviving the retry path.
- Agent row 24: Workspace Agent plan-step create/update/move/delete/approve/execute/skip/retry now also consume complete run-detail responses, so planning-mode UI mutations immediately sync run status, iteration/tool-call counts, stop reason, and tool-run approval queues without a second refresh.
- Agent row 24: Token-budget continuation now treats a resumed tool call that requires approval as a normal pending-approval pause instead of an error response; service and HTTP coverage prove `/continue-budget` clears stale budget evidence, persists the new assistant tool-call message and pending tool run, and returns complete run detail for the next operator approval.
- Agent row 24: Planning-mode token-budget continuation now reuses the failed plan-step retry path with a temporary token-budget override, so `/continue-budget` can resume a token-budget-stopped planning run without mutating the Agent's persisted budget or falling into the ReAct conversation loop.
- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28: Workspace Agent plan-step Execute and Retry controls now mirror the backend planning-mode ordering guard by staying disabled until all prior steps are completed or skipped, while direct API calls still rely on the existing backend 409 guard.
- Agent row 24: Streaming Agent messages now honor explicit runtime mode overrides in addition to the Agent's default mode: default-planning Agents can be forced through ReAct streaming, default-ReAct Agents can be forced into durable planning runs, and invalid stream mode overrides fail before message/run/plan-step persistence.
- Agent row 24: Initial planning-run generation now enforces the Agent token budget before calling the planning LLM; oversized planning prompts persist the user request plus a durable `token_budget_exceeded` planning run, but do not create assistant planning messages or plan steps.
- Agent row 24: The standalone PlanningEngine runtime now has direct unit coverage for generated-plan and adjusted-plan max-step clamping, PlanningConfig token-budget normalization, 1-based plan-step indexes, and post-step token-budget stops so runtime execution does not panic on prompt-shaped plans, accept out-of-range runtime budgets, allow unbounded dynamic replans, or report over-budget plans as completed.
- Agent row 24: The standalone PlanningEngine runtime now preserves dependency failure evidence: failed dependencies skip dependent steps with counted iteration evidence, missing dependencies avoid gateway calls, dependency-caused skips keep the plan `plan_incomplete` instead of synthesizing a completed final answer, and `GenerateAndExecutePlan` has direct coverage proving one config is applied to both max-step generation clamping and execution token-budget stops.
- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28: Real app-router regression coverage now exercises `/agent-runs/:runId/plan-steps` beyond reachability, proving run status counters, token-budget continuation controls, tool approval/reject/retry queues, operator decision reason entry, plan-step edit/insert/approve/execute/skip/delete controls, and ordering-disabled retry evidence remain reachable inside the actual Workspace shell.
- Frontend row 28: Real app-router regression coverage now goes beyond route reachability for three commercial paths: `/console/billing` proves the Console shell, sibling navigation, top-up provider controls, invoice history, and provider invoice/PDF document actions stay reachable; `/admin/billing` proves the Admin shell, recovery controls, billing tabs, filters, and table rows stay reachable; and `/marketplace/agents/:agentId` proves the Workspace shell, paid-install provider/version controls, install action, and review form stay reachable.
- Frontend row 28: A second real app-router regression slice now covers `/console/access`, `/admin/reviews`, and `/marketplace/publish`, proving API-token controls, review-SLA/decision controls, and marketplace publisher submission controls remain reachable inside the actual Console/Admin/Workspace shells rather than only through mocked route placeholders.
- Frontend row 28: Pricing and Download marketing utility pages now have direct page-level smoke tests in addition to route-manifest coverage, proving each real page renders its heading and current offer/client-selection copy without relying on mocked route placeholders.

- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28 / API contract row 31 / Security row 33: Agent planning runs now support an operator-triggered remaining-plan adjustment flow. `POST /api/v1/agent/runs/{runId}/adjust-plan` requires a reason, planning-mode ownership, cookie auth plus CSRF, preserves completed/skipped prefix evidence, replaces only the remaining pending/approved/failed suffix with newly generated plan steps, and reopens the run as `pending_approval`; the Workspace plan-step page exposes the same flow with a reason control and full run-detail refresh.
- Agent row 24: Agent planning-run adjustment now preserves audit history by saving the generated adjusted plan as an assistant message and updating `finalMessageId` before replacing remaining steps, so refreshed run detail explains why the durable plan changed instead of exposing only mutated `plan_steps` state.

- Agent row 24 / Frontend row 28: Agent planning run-level continuation now rejects non-continuable run states instead of silently refreshing terminal detail: completed, failed, max-iteration, and token-budget-stopped planning runs cannot execute remaining steps through `/continue-plan`; token-budget recovery remains isolated to `/continue-budget`, and the Workspace plan-step page disables `Continue plan` outside `pending_approval`.
